## Goal

Build TfLens: a free, multi-user, **read-only** Blazor Server lens over the telemetry TechieFlow and the AI-First-Playbook already emit (BRD §1). Users sign in through AppManager (App Id 1, every user `Manager`), add their own sources two ways — **Fetch via API** for public GitHub repos, or **Import metric files** for private and corporate ones (F-IMPORT, 2026-08-28 r2: the user uploads the telemetry their framework already writes to disk, so TfLens never needs a credential for a private repo) — and TfLens pulls or ingests the JSONL streams, archives them raw, parses them into PostgreSQL per user through **one shared parser**, and renders six report pages — Coverage, Three questions, Harness comparison, Routing & economics, **Misses & rework** and Snapshot export — each with a **Framework switch** (TechieFlow | Playbook). *(Misses & rework added by the 2026-08-28 amendment, F-MISS / BRD-112..BRD-130: a fifth stream `misses.jsonl` carrying three record kinds, whose figures must never blend measured with apportioned cost, never attribute from a guess, and never render an optional field's distribution over the whole population — Phase 3.)* The product's dangerous failure mode is a *plausible wrong number*, so the SCHEMA.md §6 provenance rules (live/backfilled · `project_type` · framework · user) are enforced in the shape of the result type with no switch to relax them, and a **mandatory parity diff against `tf-metrics.sh --rollup --json`** (BRD §13) is the acceptance gate before any figure is quotable. This single checklist is the whole app's work list — UI, functional and NFR requirements live together, distinguished only by their REQ prefix. Phase order is hard: **1 → 2 → 3**.

The `Field \| Value` header row is hidden and the columns re-split 34/66 so the email stops wrapping mid-word. **One item from the triage is WITHDRAWN:** the report claimed the copy 'before they leave the server' misstated the security property and should read 'browser', per the mockup. That was wrong — `AppManagerClient.Encrypt` (`AppManagerClient.cs:281`) does RSA-OAEP-SHA256 **server-side**; this is Blazor Server and there is no client-side WebCrypto path. The shipped copy is accurate and **`docs/mockups/profile.html` is the artefact that is wrong**. The wording was reverted and the mockup should be corrected. **2026-08-29 VERIFIED (*verify all, Release build).** Playwright 79 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped of 82 (16.4m, exit 0); .NET 638/638; render+visual gate clean across 22 screen-states / 476 controls (0 render-empty, 0 visual problems at 1280 and 390, 0 console errors). | [view](#d-req-ui-005) |

Logged and closed 2026-08-28. **⚠ UAT bug 2026-08-29 (mockup parity): **App-shell scroll containment is broken, and `/routing` is the screen that shows it.** `.tflens-page` (`MainLayout.razor:89`) is the `overflow-auto` scroll container but is **`position: static`**, so every `position:absolute` descendant resolves its containing block against `main.relative` instead and **escapes the scroller**. On `/routing` TrBlazeUI's DataTable pagination emits `sr-only` (`position:absolute`) labels — 'Go to first page' / 'Previous' / 'Next' / 'Go to last page' — that land at y=2606, dragging `document.scrollHeight` to **2607px against a 900px viewport**. Measured: `/routing` doc scrollH 2607 vs clientH 900; every other route 900/900. The visible result is a page with ~1,700px of blank void and the shell repainted at the bottom. kind: layout** (evidence: docs/mockups/*.html vs tests/.artifacts/mockup-parity/) **2026-08-29 [REQ-UI-006] FIXED (*fix-issues, mockup parity).** `.tflens-page` is now `position: relative`, so the app's scroll container is also the containing block for its absolutely-positioned descendants and TrBlazeUI's `sr-only` pagination labels can no longer escape it. **Measured across all eight routes: `document.scrollHeight` 900 against `clientHeight` 900** — `/routing` was 2607/900. | REQ-UI-013 | Remove-repo confirm dialog (BRD-101, Phase 1) | Verified | 100% | 2026-08-27 `Components/Pages/Repos.razor` Remove `AlertDialog`; purge proven at the store level. 2026-08-27 ⚠ acceptance partly unmet (*verify all): the acceptance names "Escape/Cancel aborts", but **Escape does not dismiss the remove AlertDialog** — only Cancel does (probed live on /repos: remove-title still present after Escape, gone after Cancel). Everything else passes — the dialog names the repo, Cancel leaves every row in place, remove-confirm never fires without the confirm action. Render + visual gates pass (tests/.artifacts/gates/render-visual.json). 2026-08-27 [REQ-UI-013] Escape clause closed. Root cause: TrBlazeUI 2.0.0's `AlertDialog` ships **no** Escape handling at all — its only parameters are Open/OpenChanged/DefaultOpen/OnOpenChange and, unlike `Dialog`, it is not built on `Primitives.Dialog.DialogContent`, so it never inherits that primitive's `CloseOnEscape` (logged as TR-014). Fix: new collocated module `Components/Pages/Repos.razor.js` (`watchEscape`/`stopWatchingEscape`) puts one capture-phase `keydown` listener on `document` and calls a new `[JSInvokable] DismissOpenDialogAsync` on the page, which closes the remove `AlertDialog` first, else the Connect `Dialog` when it is not mid-connect; an open `role="listbox"` popup keeps its own Escape. Page moved `IDisposable`→`IAsyncDisposable` to tear the listener and module down. Self-smoke on a live app (port 5101, Postgres 5433, `tflensdemo@techierathore.com`, Playwright headless): remove dialog on `tflenstest/StoreNullVsZero` — title "Remove tflenstest/StoreNullVsZero?" and description "Stops syncing and deletes the parsed rows and raw archive for tflenstest/StoreNullVsZero. Your GitHub repo is untouched."; **Escape** dismissed it with rows unchanged 8→8 and KPI "Connected repos 8 · 7 techieflow · 1 playbook" unchanged; Cancel likewise 8→8; confirm Remove dropped the row and moved the KPI to "Connected repos 7 · 6 techieflow · 1 playbook" with the "Removed" toast (the disposable row was restored from a DB backup afterwards); Escape also verified at 390px. Visual gate clean: no sibling overlap and no zero-size/off-viewport control at 1280 or 390, dialog open and closed, and no horizontally-overflowing pane at 390; screenshots `tests/.artifacts/requi013-dialog-desktop.png`, `-dialog-mobile.png`, `-after-remove-desktop.png`, `-page-mobile.png`; zero console errors. Durable regression added: `tests/verify/ui-auth-shell.spec.ts` → "REQ-UI-013 Escape dismisses the remove dialog and leaves every row in place" (also re-opens and Escapes a second time so a torn-down listener fails the test). Side effect, not a status change: the same listener also cures REQ-UI-012's recorded observation — that spec's `BRANCH: REQ-UI-012 Escape closed the dialog after validation` now prints `true`. Build `TfLens.slnx -c Release` 0 warnings / 0 errors; guardrails 52/52 and Core 295/295 green. 2026-08-27 VERIFIED (*verify all, chained inline from *build-phase): 55/55 Playwright acceptance tests + 425/425 .NET tests (-m:1); render gate — 343 controls across 19 screen-states, 0 RENDER-EMPTY/RENDER-ERROR; visual gate — 1280+390, 0 overlap/clip/off-viewport, 0 console errors (tests/.artifacts/gates/render-visual.json). Escape now dismisses the remove AlertDialog and leaves every row in place (pinned by ui-auth-shell.spec.ts, which Escapes twice so a torn-down listener fails); Cancel and confirm paths unchanged. Root cause was a library gap, not page code — TrBlazeUI 2.0.0 AlertDialog has no Escape handling and no CloseOnEscape (TR-014); fixed via a capture-phase keydown module in Repos.razor.js that defers to an open listbox. Side effect: the same fix cured REQ-UI-012's recorded post-validation Escape defect. 2026-08-28 ⚠ UAT report (owner): 'deleting a repo disabled the whole page' — **COULD NOT REPRODUCE** (evidence: docs/uatissuessc/Repos-delete-issue.png). Driven live on /repos at 1999x1080 across nine variants: open dialog; Cancel; Escape; outside-click; CONFIRMED delete (rows 4→3, page stayed fully interactive, `sync-now` clickable, 0 console/pageerror); and with each of `TfLens.styles.css`, `app.css`, `trblazeui.css`, `portal.js` and the server process removed. None reproduce the owner's evidence, which shows the `bg-black/80` backdrop mounted with NO dialog panel. Blocking `portal.js` yields neither backdrop nor panel; killing the server yields no overlay. **Untested variable: the owner's actual browser (Edge on Windows) — every run here was headless Chromium.** No status change; see the OWNER QUESTION in PROJECT-STATUS. Unchanged blocker: needs a reset token issued by AppManager for App 1. 2026-08-27 [REQ-FN-003] PARTIAL -> 85%. **Owner-gated only on the live round trip; everything else is now closed with durable tests.** (1) *Non-credential scoping re-probed and exhausted.* The guide (§3.1, `/AuthSvc/forgot-password` and `/AuthSvc/reset-password`) states the ApplicationId is required **via `X-Api-Key`** for these two endpoints specifically, unlike `/AuthSvc/register` (body) and `/LicenseSvc/types` (query). Re-probed live against `https://appmgrapi.techierathore.com`: body `applicationId`, body `aApplicationId`, `?aApplicationId`, `?applicationId`, header `X-App-Id`, header `X-Application-Id` and a form-encoded body **all** answer `400 APPLICATION_ID_REQUIRED`; a control call `GET /LicenseSvc/types?aApplicationId=1` returns `200`, proving query resolution works elsewhere and is simply not wired on these two endpoints. Also confirmed the server decrypts `encryptedNewPassword` *before* resolving scope, so even a correctly RSA-OAEP-SHA256-encrypted reset reaches `APPLICATION_ID_REQUIRED`. (2) *Enumeration safety — proven structurally, not just visually.* `tests/TfLens.Integration.Tests/PasswordResetEndpointTests.cs` compares real HTTP responses for the demo address and an unknown one and asserts status, `Location`, body and every non-volatile header are identical; `ResetTokenHygieneTests` proves the handler has no `if`/`catch`/`switch` and exactly one `return` after the AppManager call, so no answer — and no time taken to answer — can steer the response; `PasswordResetTests` proves the client swallows `USER_NOT_FOUND`, `APPLICATION_ID_REQUIRED`, `INVALID_API_KEY`, `VALIDATION_ERROR`, `INTERNAL_ERROR` and a transport failure, and sends byte-identical requests for both addresses. (3) *`INVALID_RESET_TOKEN` and `APP_ID_MISMATCH` collapse to one outcome* — proven with a stubbed AppManager for each code at both layers: identical redirect `/reset-password?error=expired` and identical response bytes at the endpoint, identically shaped `AppManagerException` at the client. (4) *Two real leaks found and fixed.* **(a)** `ResetPassword.razor`'s interactive form declared no `action`, so Blazor stamped the *request URL* into it — the emailed link, token and all, in the server-rendered page source. Fixed with a static `action="/reset-password"`, guarded by `ResetTokenHygieneTests.TheResetFormDeclaresAStaticActionSoTheLinkIsNotStampedIntoIt`. **(b)** ASP.NET Core hosting diagnostics logged the whole request URL, writing a live reset link into the console and the 14-day rolling file twice per visit (20 hits on one smoke run). Fixed with `src/TfLens/Services/Auth/ResetTokenRedaction.cs`, a Serilog enricher attached at the logger in `Program.cs`; `ResetTokenLogRedactionTests` proves the framework template is redacted, that path/status survive, and that `refreshToken=`/`antiforgeryToken=` are deliberately untouched. (5) *Token never logged* — proven at runtime (capturing logger over success, both dead-link codes, weak-password, 500, rotated-key retry and transport failure) and by source scan of every `.cs`/`.razor` under `src/`, reading whole multi-line call expressions. (6) *Empty-credential handling confirmed correct and pinned:* `.env` holds both keys as empty strings; `Validate()` treats an empty pair as unconfigured (starts) and a half pair as fatal, and `HasAppManagerApiCredentials` is false so **no empty header is ever sent** — `ApiCredentialConfigurationTests` covers `""`, `"   "` and `null` in every combination. Smoke: app on :5105 + Postgres :5433, `tests/verify/fn-password-reset.spec.ts` 7/7 green — identical outcome for `tflensdemo@techierathore.com` vs an unknown address, no token in DOM/attributes/serialised HTML, one "invalid or has expired" sentence for all three dead-link routes, visual gate clean at 1280 and 390 (`tests/.artifacts/{forgot,reset}-password-{1280,390}.png`); app log confirmed to contain `?token=[redacted]` and neither address. Build 0 warnings, `dotnet test TfLens.slnx -c Release` **409/409** green. **Remaining (owner-gated, ~15%):** the live round trip — AppManager actually mailing a reset link and that link completing a password change — needs `TfLensAppManagerApiKey` + `TfLensAppManagerApiSecret` for Application 1. Nothing else about this requirement is unproven. 2026-08-27 [REQ-FN-003] ⚠ TWO REAL TOKEN LEAKS FOUND AND FIXED — the earlier "never echoes its token" claim had only checked input values. (1) ResetPassword.razor's interactive form declared no `action`, so Blazor stamped the request URL — the emailed reset link, token and all — into the server-rendered page source. (2) ASP.NET Core hosting diagnostics logged the full request URL, writing a live reset link to the console and the 14-day rolling file twice per visit (20 hits in one smoke run); no care in TfLens's own code could prevent it, so it is fixed with a Serilog enricher that rewrites only the token parameter. All three acceptance clauses are now proven by 53 new tests: enumeration-safety is byte-identical at the endpoint and through the browser, both INVALID_RESET_TOKEN and APP_ID_MISMATCH collapse to one "invalid or expired" outcome (scripted AppManager), and the token never reaches a log (real Serilog pipeline + a source scan of every .cs/.razor). STILL PARTIAL — OWNER-GATED: the requirement's own mechanism, the API-key header supplying the app scope, cannot be exercised. Re-probed live this pass: body applicationId, body aApplicationId, ?aApplicationId, ?applicationId, X-App-Id, X-Application-Id and a form-encoded body ALL return 400 APPLICATION_ID_REQUIRED, while a control GET /LicenseSvc/types?aApplicationId=1 returns 200. Needs TfLensAppManagerApiKey/Secret (both present but empty in .env). 2026-08-27 VERIFIED (*verify all — re-run after the owner supplied the AppManager API credentials): the blocker is GONE. With the pair configured, `/AuthSvc/forgot-password` answers **200** live through the running app (it answered `400 APPLICATION_ID_REQUIRED` for the whole project until now) and `/AuthSvc/reset-password` reaches **real token validation** (`INVALID_RESET_TOKEN`) instead of dying on scope — so the requirement's own mechanism, the API-key header supplying the app scope, is now exercised end to end. All three acceptance clauses hold: enumeration-safety is byte-identical at the endpoint and through the browser; `INVALID_RESET_TOKEN` is proven LIVE and `APP_ID_MISMATCH` by scripted response (AppManager validates the token before comparing app ids, so it cannot be reached for this endpoint — both collapse to the one "invalid or expired" outcome either way); the token never reaches a log. 21/21 auth+reset Playwright specs and 427/427 .NET tests green. ⚠ ONE FIX WAS REQUIRED TO MAKE THE KEYS SAFE: sending the pair on every call broke `/UserSvc/profile` with `403 NO_APP_ACCESS` (measured: 200 without the pair, 403 with it). The pair now goes to `/AuthSvc/*` only — an application credential has no business on a read the bearer token already scopes — pinned by two tests in `ApiCredentialConfigurationTests`. Root cause of that 403 is an AppManager-side grant (the demo account has no access row for Application 1, the same gap that makes its `applicationRole` empty); see Known blockers. REMAINING (owner UAT, not a code gap): completing a password change with a token from a real reset email needs mailbox access. 2026-08-29 re-verified (*verify all, Release build): Playwright 79 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped of 82 (16.4m, exit 0); .NET 638/638; render+visual gate clean across 22 screen-states / 476 controls (0 render-empty, 0 visual problems at 1280 and 390, 0 console errors). | [view](#d-req-fn-003) |

Random 256-bit session id; cookie claims are `tflens:sid`, `tflens:uid`, `ClaimTypes.Email/Name/Role=Manager` (the shape `CurrentUser` reads). Root cause is MetricsEngine.DeclaredProjectType (src/TfLens.Core/Metrics/MetricsEngine.cs:207): it takes .FirstOrDefault() over gates, then runs, then sessions, then commits with NO ordering by timestamp, so it returns whichever record the store happens to hand back first. Its own doc comment claims it mirrors the reference reading core-config.yaml, but the reference reads the repository's CURRENT declaration. Evidence in the records: 'app' is the newest project_type in every one of the five streams (runs, gates, sessions, commits all newest 2026-08-29; misses are 100 percent app) while 'docs' stops at 2026-08-28. Gates is the ONLY stream where docs outnumbers app (225 vs 180), which is consistent with a first-or-majority read rather than a newest read. Owner-visible effect: snapshot.md line 17 prints project type 'docs' for TfLens and line 24 warns it 'has records still declaring app', telling a reader the opposite of the truth in a document whose whole purpose is to be quotable. Why it was never caught: the 2026-08-29T06:01 parity run's dataset was TechieBlog, TechieFlow, TechieRag, TrBlazeUI — it did not include TfLens, the only reclassified repository, which BRD section 16 predicted would 'hit this case first'. Evidence tests/.artifacts/parity/reference-postpurge.json vs tflens-postpurge.json. NOT fixed here — verify does not edit source. **2026-08-29 [REQ-FN-058] FIXED — the project-type inversion is gone.** `MetricsEngine.DeclaredProjectType` no longer takes `.FirstOrDefault()` over an unordered concat of the four streams. It now pairs each record's declared type with its `Ts` instant (all four record types carry `Ts`), ranks them newest-first and returns the newest declaration — the repository's CURRENT classification, which is what the reference reads out of `core-config.yaml`. Determinism is explicit rather than incidental: a record whose `Ts` is blank or unparseable never outranks one with a usable instant, and records sharing an instant (or all lacking one) are broken by ordinal comparison of the declared value itself, lowest first — a rule that reads nothing but the values in hand, so the answer cannot change with the order the store returns rows in. Timestamps are compared as instants, not as text, so the same moment written in two offsets is a tie. The XML doc comment was rewritten to say this and why, and keeps the `<remarks>` note that the declared type is a coverage fact and never a segment key — `Segment.KeyFor` is untouched and an inferred record still segments as `unclassified` (REQ-FN-048, asserted by a test). `SnapshotExporter.RepoOriginsFor` derives `stale_types` as every distinct type that is not `PerRepoFacts.ProjectType`, so the one fix corrects both keys and no export code was touched. Ten new tests in `tests/TfLens.Core.Tests/Metrics/DeclaredProjectTypeTests.cs` — the explicit TfLens regression (225 older `docs` gates against 180 newer `app` ones, plus the same older-vs-newer split in runs, sessions and commits, expecting `app`), the same case re-seeded in the opposite row order to prove order-independence, one-type-only, newest-record-in-another-stream-than-the-majority, the empty/all-null fallback to `app`, same-instant tie-break asserted both seed orders, offsets compared as instants, an undated record losing to a dated one, all-undated still deterministic, and declared-type-is-not-a-segment-key. Build `dotnet build TfLens.slnx -c Release` PASS 0 warnings 0 errors. Top-level keys are exactly per_repo, tainted_reqs, live, backfilled, pooled, misses, extras, parity, so `extras` and `parity` remain the only additions the reference does not emit and `tools/parity-compare.py` still walks the reference's keys with no mapping layer. **BRD section 13 compare re-run WITH TfLens in the dataset** (`tools/parity-compare.py tests/.artifacts/parity/reference-postpurge.json <fresh export> --allow-environment-keys`): **3 findings before, 1 after**. They inflated TechieFlow to 34 gates against 0 upstream and TrBlazeUI to 43 against 8. Purged (backup kept); every stored count then matched upstream exactly. Same class as the documented `acme/*` and `tflenstest/Store*` pollution — see the new REQ-NFR-019. **(2) CODE — cost attribution was read from storage, not derived.** The framework's 17:04 oracle rewrite began recomputing `cost_attribution` per fix run (SCHEMA.md §8: a derived metric is computed at report time). TfLens still trusted the stored string, which is wrong for two reasons the stream proves about itself — it is stamped one record at a time, so a run closing four misses writes `shared:1..shared:4` and only the last is right; and pre-2026-08-28 records carry `none` from the empty-`reqs_touched` bug. `MissFigures.MoneyFor` now recomputes from the miss ids each `fix_run_id` closed, and emits the oracle's new `cost_recovered_n`. **(3) CODE — `session_duplicates_collapsed` counted history, not data.** `RepoSyncRunner` stored `SessionsPresented - inserted` **accumulated across syncs**, so re-syncing an unchanged file grew the figure by a whole file each time: TechieFlow reached **25** against the **3** duplicates its file actually contains. A figure that changes with how many times you read the data is not quotable — two instances on the same repos would disagree. Now assigns the current snapshot's own count, which is what `dedupe_sessions` measures. **(4) TEST — the spec hard-coded the stamp's date and digest**, so it went red on every legitimate re-pin (twice in two days). It now reads `data/parity-last.json` and asserts the page agrees with the record — the property the REQ actually names. Evidence: `tools/parity-compare.py … --allow-environment-keys` → **exit 0, 0 findings, 24 allowed differences**; stamp `src/TfLens/data/parity-last.json` date 2026-08-29, script hash `sha256:12953226…`, dataset SHAs pinned; `parity-gate-smoke.spec.ts` **3/3**; **638/638 .NET**. 2026-08-29 re-verified (*verify all, Release build): Playwright 79 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped of 82 (16.4m, exit 0); .NET 638/638; render+visual gate clean across 22 screen-states / 476 controls (0 render-empty, 0 visual problems at 1280 and 390, 0 console errors). | [view](#d-req-fn-063) |

Nothing surfaced the switch, and the reason it could not is the real defect: **the same connection string was hard-coded in FIVE places** — `TfLensOptions.LocalDevelopmentConnection` (seeded as a Development config source), `PostgresFixture.LocalDefault`, and three separate `const`s in `MissStoreTests`, `PostgresStoreTests` and `RepoRegistryTests` — all naming `Port=5433` with `Password=tflensdev` inline. | REQ-NFR-012 | Shared AppManager test accounts are provisioned and restorable (owner report 2026-08-28, Phase 1) | Verified | 100% | 2026-08-28 [REQ-NFR-012] logged via *log-miss 2026-08-28 — `MISS-TfLens-20260828-02` (`unspecified-gap` / `tests` / `blocker`). **No requirement has ever owned how the live AppManager demo accounts are created, credentialled or restored**, so the authenticated half of the suite rests on mutable external state that nothing in this repository can rebuild. It has now failed: AppManager rejects both accounts documented in `docs/TfLens-UsageGuide.md` with `INVALID_CREDENTIALS (401)`, taking 7 tests down (5 `AppManagerLiveTests` + the 2 `CrossUserIsolationTests` sign-ins) and leaving every authenticated REQ un-reverifiable. The cause is *not* the API key pair — `AppManagerLiveTests.BuildClient()` sends `new TfLensOptions()` with no pair at all and fails identically, and the code is always `INVALID_CREDENTIALS`, never `INVALID_API_KEY`. Which run rotated the password could not be established from `runs.jsonl`, so no origin run is claimed. Owner action required, then `*amend-docs` to give this a BRD clause. **BUILT 2026-08-28 [REQ-NFR-012].** Four pieces. (1) **A restore tool that was proven by running it** — `provision-test-accounts`, a new verb on the existing CLI (`CommandRunner`, alongside `rebuild`/`sync`/`export`; optional `--guide <path>`). It parses the UsageGuide Test-users table, signs each account in with the recorded password and — only if that fails — registers it with `applicationRoleCode: "Manager"`, then reports each `userId` and `applicationRole`. Idempotent (an account that works is left untouched) and prints no password. Run live: `dotnet run --project src/TfLens -- provision-test-accounts` → `tflensdemo@techierathore.com already usable userId 2 applicationRole 'Manager'` / `tflenstest2@techierathore.com already usable userId 3 applicationRole 'Manager'` / `2 of 2 documented accounts usable`, **exit 0**, and re-running gave byte-identical output. The one case it cannot self-repair — the address exists under a different password, `DUPLICATE_EMAIL` — it names in full rather than failing opaquely, because that is the exact state that caused this miss. (2) **The table is now read mechanically**, not merely by people: `TestAccountRegistry` (TfLens.Core) is the one parser, shared by the verb and the guardrails. (3) **Four guardrail tests** in `tests/TfLens.Guardrails.Tests/TestAccountRegistryTests` — the table parses into ≥2 usable accounts; **every live `@techierathore.com` account the suite names appears in the table** (the acceptance clause, scoped to the *table* rather than the whole guide so a retired account's farewell note cannot excuse a test that still uses it); no `[Trait("Category","Live")]` test mutates a password without a following `LoginAsync` restore proof; and the guide documents the verb. Guardrails 58/59 (was 55/55 + 4 new). (4) **Password-mutation audit** — the suite was swept for ChangePassword/ResetPassword against a shared account. Only `AppManagerLiveTests.ChangePasswordRejectsWrongCurrent` reaches the live API, and it already passes the *wrong* current password on purpose and re-signs-in with the documented one afterwards, so it cannot rotate anything; everything else (`AppManagerClientTests`, `PasswordResetTests`) runs against a scripted transport. **No test was found that could have caused the rotation** — which is why the guardrail pins the property going forward rather than a fix being applied to an offender. Docs: UsageGuide Test-users table rewritten to current truth (rows 1 and 2 now record `applicationRole: Manager` as actually returned; row 4 `tflensrole@` removed — it existed only as the AM-001 reproduction case and no longer exists in AppManager), with the restore procedure documented directly beneath it. ⚠ **Status stops at `Implemented`, not `Verified`** (self-attestation) and at 85%, not 100%: the acceptance clause "`dotnet test TfLens.slnx -c Release` returns to green" is **not met at the time of writing, for reasons outside this REQ** — concurrent clusters were still landing the import and miss-stream work throughout this run (Core.Tests grew 338 → 421 tests while it was in progress, and the solution twice failed to compile mid-edit). Final full run `~/.dotnet/dotnet test TfLens.slnx -c Release -m:1`: **Core 418/421** (red: `TelemetryImportServiceTests.NoImportOnlyParseDedupeOrUpsertExists`, `ParityStampTests.HashingAgreesWithTheDigestTheRecorderWrites`, `PostgresStoreTests.RebuildReproducesLiveSyncCounts`), **Guardrails 63/64** (red: `DocumentationTests.ReadmeCarriesTheOutOfScopeListVerbatim`, because `docs/TfLens-BRD.md` §3 was amended for **Import metric files** at 10:54 and README's verbatim copy has not been re-synced), **Integration 43/43 PASS**. Every one of those four belongs to another cluster's in-flight REQ and none touches identity — all **four new guardrail tests pass**, and the **76 `AppManager` tests pass, live ones included** (`--filter FullyQualifiedName~AppManager` → 76/76). One dialog, two modes: `Tabs` `source-mode` with `source-mode-api` / `source-mode-import` (TR-010 — the trigger takes no attributes, so each id sits on a span inside it), panels `source-panel-api` / `source-panel-import`. Import mode carries `import-name`, `import-framework`, `import-project-type`, drop zone `import-drop` (drag-drop **and** `Choose files`), `import-preview` (+ `import-preview-streams`, `import-bundle-sha`, `import-preview-summary`) and `import-submit`, which is disabled until a preview lands. The bytes go straight from the browser to `POST /api/import/preview` / `/api/import/commit` as multipart with a Blazor `<AntiforgeryToken />` value, so a 25 MB bundle never crosses the SignalR circuit and the endpoints' own pre-read size gate is what bounds it; several loose `.jsonl` files are packed into one **stored** zip in the browser so the upload has a single sha256 (ADR-022). Refusals render `ImportRefusal.Message` **verbatim**. BRD-100's private-repo refusal now carries `connect-switch-import`, which switches mode inline and carries the typed repo across. | REQ-NFR-016 | `bin/` and `obj/` must not be tracked in git (owner report 2026-08-28, UAT) | Verified | 100% | logged from UAT 2026-08-28. PROVEN this session: `.gitignore` has no `bin/`/`obj/` entry and `git ls-files src/TfLens/bin` lists build output, so `TfLens.staticwebassets.runtime.json` is committed carrying **machine-absolute** static-web-asset content roots. Building in WSL writes `/mnt/c/…` + `/home/srkra/.nuget/…`; building the same commit from Windows rewrites them to `C:\1MyCode\…` + `C:\Users\srkra\.nuget\…` (both captured this session). Shipping that file between machines is the mechanism by which a static web asset can 404 on one developer's box and not another's, which is the class of failure behind REQ-UI-001. Also makes every build dirty the working tree (34+ files at session start). **2026-08-28 [REQ-NFR-016] PARTIALLY FIXED (*fix-issues).** `.gitignore` now carries `[Bb]in/` and `[Oo]bj/`, with the reason recorded in the file, and `UatEscapeTests.BuildOutputIsGitIgnored` pins it (asserted on `.gitignore` rather than by shelling out to git, so it is hermetic). **OWNER ACTION STILL REQUIRED — agents never run git:** the already-tracked files must be untracked once, by hand — `git rm -r --cached src/TfLens/bin src/TfLens/obj src/TfLens.Core/bin src/TfLens.Core/obj tests/*/bin tests/*/obj` then commit. Until that runs, machine-absolute manifests remain in history and the working tree keeps going dirty on every build. Cannot reach Verified before that step. 2026-08-28 verifier: `UatEscapeTests.BuildOutputIsGitIgnored` passes. **Cannot reach `Verified` until the owner runs the one-off `git rm -r --cached` above — agents never run git, so the acceptance clause 'git ls-files returns zero paths under bin/ or obj/' is not assertable from here.** **2026-08-29 ROOT CAUSE LOCATED, AND IT IS OURS (owner asked who; the first answer given was a deflection and is corrected here).** The agent running day-1 wrote this file and did not read it. `.gitignore` was created by the day-1 scaffold (`979265f`, 2026-08-26) with a complete set of sections for the FRAMEWORK's artifacts (`.tfcore/`, `node_modules/`, `tests/.artifacts/`, `playwright-report/`, `logs/`) and **no section for the project's own stack** — no `bin/`, no `obj/`, in a repo whose `core-config.yaml` and four `.csproj` files say .NET 10. The first build then produced artefacts and commit **`80cb71c` — named "Updated git ignore" (2026-08-27) — staged 1,041 of them**; four later commits took it to 1,962. Attribution is the **day-1 agent** (`day1-greenfield`), which is what `MISS-TfLens-20260828-21` already records as its `origin_phase` — that record stands and is NOT duplicated (SCHEMA.md §5.5.4). **The template is a contributing cause, not the responsible party.** An agent that generates a file is responsible for reading it, and this one had itself just chosen .NET 10, written `core-config.yaml` and scaffolded four `.csproj` files — it had every fact needed to notice the omission. 'The scaffold omitted it' was offered as the answer first and was wrong: the scaffold is a tool this agent operates, not a colleague it can hand the blame to. Upgraded upstream: **TF-007 item 1** now carries this as a located root cause with the commit evidence, and asks that whichever day-1 step emits `.gitignore` emit the stack's build-output rules with it. Owner action unchanged: `bash scripts/untrack-build-output.sh --run`. **2026-08-29 VERIFIED — the owner ran `scripts/untrack-build-output.sh --run`.** `git ls-files` now returns **0** paths under any `bin/` or `obj/` directory (was 1,962), and `UatEscapeTests.BuildOutputIsGitIgnored` passes. The acceptance clauses are met in the index; the **owner's commit is what makes it permanent** — until then a reset would restore the tracking. **A second defect surfaced in the handover itself and is logged as `MISS-TfLens-20260829-04`:** the script did its job but reported success in a way that read as failure — removing 1,962 files from the index stages 1,962 deletions, so Visual Studio then lists every one of them under 'Changes to be committed', which is indistinguishable from 'the folders are still staged' unless you read the change type on each row. The owner reasonably concluded the script had done nothing. Nobody had run it end to end and looked at what the operator would see afterwards (`why_missed: insufficient-verify-method`). The script now names which of three states the repository is in, verifies the end state itself, and warns about the staged-deletions list before the reader opens their editor. 2026-08-29 re-verified (*verify all, Release build): Playwright 79 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped of 82 (16.4m, exit 0); .NET 638/638; render+visual gate clean across 22 screen-states / 476 controls (0 render-empty, 0 visual problems at 1280 and 390, 0 console errors). | [view](#d-req-nfr-016) |

| REQ-NFR-019 | Stored provenance is real: no row claims a `source_sha` that is not a commit in its repository (BRD-143, found 2026-08-29 by the BRD §13 parity re-run) | Implemented | 85% | 2026-08-29 [REQ-NFR-019] **Now BRD-owned** — `*amend-docs` appended **BRD-143** (§11, amendment 2026-08-29) before this REQ was built, so the requirement is owned rather than inferred from a finding. §13 gained a second standing rule (a passing parity diff alone is not a licence to quote — provenance the store never obtained is invisible to the diff until the counts happen to disagree) and §14 gained the matching done-criterion. Origin of the REQ follows. logged 2026-08-29. Found by the parity re-run: 155 rows across `Gate`/`Run`/`Session`/`Commit` carried two `SourceSha` values that **do not exist** in their repositories (`a91f3c2e…`, `e3b9d40a…` — both HTTP 422 from the GitHub commits API, both hand-typed sequential hex, alongside the literal `smokesha` under test user 9001). They were seeded directly into the store, bypassing the sync path, and they inflated TechieFlow's gate count to 34 against 0 upstream. **This is worse than a parity annoyance:** `source_sha` is what BRD §13 pins a quotable figure to and what `/export` publishes as dataset identity, so a row with invented provenance makes an exported number unreproducible by the person checking it — the precise failure BRD §1 names. Purged this session, but nothing stops it recurring. **2026-08-29 *verify all: NOT IMPLEMENTED — confirmed absent, and the pollution it exists to catch is live right now.** (a) No check of any kind exists: nothing under tests/, tools/ or src/ compares a stored SourceSha against SyncState or an import bundle. (b) Queried the running store directly (WinPostgre, database tflens): **155 rows under the real user 2 carry a SourceSha that no sync ever obtained** — a91f3c2e4b7d9018f5c6a2b3d4e5f60718293a4b on techierathore/TechieFlow (77 rows across Run, Gate, Session, Commit) and e3b9d40a1c2b3d4e5f60718293a4b5c6d7e8f901 on techierathore/TrBlazeUI (78 rows). Both contain the hand-typed nibble run d4e5f60718293a4b; neither is a git hash. That is the same 155 the 2026-08-29 parity re-run recorded purging, so **the purge did not reach the database the app now reads** (it ran before the migration to WinPostgre). The raw archive was poisoned too (8 .jsonl files under src/TfLens/data/raw/2/ for the two SHAs), so a targeted DELETE would have been silently undone by the next rebuild; those files were removed FIRST, then the app's own rebuild --user 2 replayed the clean archive (34 files, 1101 records, 0 invalid lines) and RecomputeSyncCountsAsync rebuilt the SyncState counters from reality. Backup of all 155 rows plus the 8 raw files kept outside the repo. Consequences found: TechieFlow's GatesCount went 34 to 0 — every gate row it had was fabricated (both surviving gates files are genuinely 0 lines), TrBlazeUI lost 35 of 43, and the whole 'framework' project-type tab disappeared from /three-questions because it had no real gate records behind it. THE REQUIREMENT ITSELF IS STILL UNBUILT — this was a cleanup, not the check REQ-NFR-019 asks for; status stays Planned 0 percent. **2026-08-29 [REQ-NFR-019] BUILT (Implemented, not Verified).** Four pieces, all against the running app. (1) *Write path:* `Core/Provenance/ProvenanceRules.RequireObtained` is called from `StreamParser.Parse` — the one door every stream row passes through (sync, import, rebuild replay, Playbook adapter) — so a blank `SourceSha` is a `ProvenanceException`, not an empty string; and `database/001-schema.sql` adds a `Ck<Table>SourceShaPresent` CHECK to all eight stream tables, so raw SQL (the layer the 155 rows actually arrived through) is refused too. Confirmed live: all 8 constraints exist in the running DB and a hand-written INSERT with a blank SHA returned `23514`. (2) *Harness:* clause 2 is met by its SECOND half — `Core/Provenance/ReservedUserIds` formalises the band at and above 90000 that `Fixtures.cs` already used by hand, and `SnapshotExporter.ExportAsync` refuses it outright, so seeded data has no published figure by construction. `tests/.artifacts/harness/seed-metrics-smoke.sql` moved 9001 to 90001 and now declares its own provenance. (3) *Check:* new `"SourceProvenance"` ledger, written by the sync at fetch time and by the import at commit time; `ProvenanceAudit.Compare` reports any stored `(user, repo, SourceSha)` that matches no ledger entry, no `SyncState.LastSha`, no `UserRepo.BundleSha` and no raw-archive file name — **no network call, no count comparison**. Surfaced as `dotnet TfLens.dll provenance-check [--user <id>]`, exit 1 on a finding, and it has no ignore/skip/force switch. New `provenance-check` verb (exit 1 on a finding, no ignore/skip/force switch, guardrail-asserted by reflection per BRD-89). Clause 4 lands as ParityReasons.ProvenanceOrphan inside parity.status_reason, called by BOTH SnapshotExporter and ExportSurface.razor so the page and the file cannot disagree - no new top-level key, so REQ-FN-058's layout is intact and observed intact. 31 new test methods across 4 files. Detection PROVEN end to end, not asserted: clean check (11 sources over 1139 rows, 0 unaccounted) then a fabricated a91f3c2e row injected under documented test user 3 (never user 2) then ORPHAN reported and exit 1 then /export NOT QUOTABLE with the new reason at 1280 and 390 then rows removed then QUOTABLE again. Consequence: **a poisoned raw jsonl replayed by rebuild is still accounted for, which is half of what actually happened on 2026-08-29.** The ledger closes this going forward, not retrospectively. (b) A store that cannot audit (in-memory fixture) returns Unsupported and therefore does not refuse QUOTABLE - a fail-open, and BRD-89 says integrity rules have no relaxation. (c) Clause 2 is met by its second half only: the harness can still write into the app's own store, but ReservedUserIds (>= 90000) is formalised and SnapshotExporter refuses that band outright. | REQ-NFR-020 | A built screen is graded against its approved mockup, mechanically (BRD-144, found 2026-08-29 by the owner's mockup-parity UAT) | Implemented | 90% | 2026-08-29 [REQ-NFR-020] **Now BRD-owned** — `*amend-docs` appended **BRD-144** (§11, amendment 2026-08-29) before this REQ was built. F-OPS reopened to `Partial` in BRD §4 to carry it, and §14 gained the matching done-criterion. Origin of the REQ follows. logged 2026-08-29. The owner compared all 18 screens in `docs/mockups/` against the running app and found structural drift on **13 of the 14 comparable screens**, against a checklist that read **145 Verified**. Every one of those screens had passed acceptance, the §4a data-render gate and the §4b visual-truth gate — because **no gate in the framework compares a built screen to its approved design**. §4a asks 'does the control show data?'; §4b asks 'do controls overlap or leave the viewport?'. A badge rendered as plain text has text and does not overlap. A header that wraps to two rows does not overlap. A 71px value column that splits `2,287,975,139` across three lines does not overlap. A missing icon is nothing to measure. All of them pass, and all of them are wrong. **This is the single root cause behind 18 of the 20 findings**, and it is the same shape as TF-007 (no asset-integrity gate, found the same way one day earlier): the gate set measures liveness, not fidelity. Raised upstream as **TF-008**. **2026-08-29 *verify all: NOT IMPLEMENTED — confirmed absent.** No mockup-parity gate exists anywhere: no spec under tests/verify/ loads docs/mockups/, and no tool under tools/ compares a built page against one. The 18 mockups are present and the visual gate does run at 1280 and 390, but it asserts geometry and console cleanliness only — it cannot see a badge rendered as plain text, a missing icon, a wrong semantic colour or a clipped column, which is the whole class this REQ was raised for. Left at Planned 0% for the same reason as REQ-NFR-019. This is the root cause behind the 2026-08-29 drift finding and it recurs until it exists. **2026-08-29 [REQ-NFR-020] IMPLEMENTED — the `mockup-parity` gate now exists and has been seen to fail.** `tests/verify/gates-mockup-parity.spec.ts` + `tests/verify/_mockup-parity.ts`, black-box (no application source touched), sibling to the §4a/§4b sweep and following its conventions. It loads each `docs/mockups/*.html` in the same browser as the app, reduces BOTH sides to a structural signature anchored on `data-testid` (with a positional `th`/`td` path inside each named table, since neither side anchors cells) and diffs the signatures at 1280 and 390 — deliberately NOT a pixel diff, which over hand-drawn mockups produces the noise that gets a gate switched off (REQ-NFR-018). Six graded classes: badge/pill rendered as plain text, missing icon, semantic colour bucket, wrap where the mockup is single-line, clipped out of container, and value cell narrower than its longest unbreakable token (measured with the element's own font on a canvas, never eyeballed). Clause 2 (`scrollHeight <= clientHeight + 2`) runs over every route independently of any mockup. Route↔mockup map taken from BRD §9 Screen inventory, not guessed from filenames. **First real run: 21 screens — 2 PASS, 10 FAIL (38 findings), 6 SKIPPED (Playbook axis measured at 0 repos at run time, so the skip lifts by itself when a Playbook repo is connected), 3 ⚠ NO-MOCKUP** (`/not-found`, `/repos/add`, `/repos/add/import`). Findings file `tests/.artifacts/gates/mockup-parity.json`. **Catch proof** (`tests/.artifacts/gates/mockup-parity-catch-proof.json`): an injected structural difference on an element the two sides currently agree on makes the gate FAIL for badge, icon and token, and removing the injection makes it quiet again — 0 → 1 → 0 findings on each. Allow-list is narrow and per-element (wildcard testids banned, max 6 entries per screen, and every entry that matched nothing is reported as UNUSED so a waiver cannot rot); 1 of 8 entries currently fires. **Not yet at 100%:** clause 3 needs the orchestrator to wire the gate into `verify-phase` and map its findings file into `gates_run` as `mockup-parity` — `.tfcore/` is externally managed and REQ-NFR-018 forbids this repository pinning files there, so that wiring is reported upstream, not done here. Ceiling is `Implemented`; the 38 findings are the honest measurement of what remains after the 2026-08-29 repairs, not a gate to be tuned until it reports zero. 2026-08-29 [REQ-NFR-020] **BUILT (BRD-144) - Implemented, NOT Verified; clause 3 unwired.** New black-box gate tests/verify/gates-mockup-parity.spec.ts plus _mockup-parity.ts: structural signature per element (badge/pill chrome, icon presence, quantised semantic colour bucket, wrap, clip, and longest-unbreakable-token vs cell width), anchored on data-testid with a positional th/td path inside named tables. Deliberately NOT a pixel diff - noise over hand-drawn mockups trains people to ignore the gate. Clause 1 MET, clause 2 MET (built and proved first, 30 route/width pairs measured, 5 FAIL), clause 4 MET (3 routes report NO-MOCKUP, 6 Playbook screens SKIPPED with a MEASURED reason that lifts by itself when a Playbook repo is connected - not a hard-coded list). **Clause 3 PARTLY MET and this is the whole reason for 90 percent:** the gate runs in the normal playwright sweep and writes tests/.artifacts/gates/mockup-parity.json, but nothing yet emits a per-screen gates_run row, so a screen can still reach Verified on render-truth and visual-truth alone. Wiring it needs a change under .tfcore/, which REQ-NFR-018 forbids this repository from making - raised upstream as TF-008. **The gate proved it can catch, which is the part that matters:** for each of icon, badge and token it took an element the two sides currently AGREE on, broke one property in the browser, and asserted findings went 0 to 1 to 0 across the revert. That catch-proof found a real bug in the gate itself - a cell crushed to 18px returned null instead of the worst-possible overflow, so it had been silently passing the most broken column it could meet. Four false-positive classes were found and fixed during the run (131 findings down to 44), each a correctness fix with its reasoning recorded in source, not a threshold moved; the largest was that oklch() and color-mix() were unparseable, which had silently disabled the colour clause on the app side because the app is Tailwind v4. **First-run result: 1 PASS, 11 FAIL, 6 SKIPPED, 3 NO-MOCKUP, 44 findings, 8 waived, 2 unattributable.** Honest limits: chart series colours are not graded (canvas/SVG carry no per-series anchor on either side, and BRD-144 names them); positional td paths compare different records, which is sound for token and clip but content-dependent for cell-level wrap; and the Playbook axis is unproven rather than passing. 7 of the 8 allow-list entries report UNUSED because the three documented deviations are out of the gate's reach - coverage.html and three-questions.html carry no kpi-* testids and profile.html no profile-identity-note. Entries kept and reported as covering nothing, so a waiver cannot rot. | [view](#d-req-nfr-020) |


**Status values:** `Not Started` · `In Progress` · `Implemented` (code done, not yet verified) · `Verified` (self-smoke or verifier PASS — acceptance AND data-render AND visual gates all pass) · `Done (pre-existing)` (migrated from an earlier dev plan as already complete — build agents must NOT rebuild; terminal like `Verified`) · `Needs re-verify` (a defect or change was logged — must be re-run before it can return to `Verified`) · `PARTIAL` (some acceptance unmet — say what in Remarks) · `FAIL` (verifier ran and failed — bug in Remarks) · `Blocked` (external/library gap — link the TR-/TR-RAG- entry in Remarks) · `N/A`.

**% guide:** `0` not started · `25` scaffolded · `50` in progress · `75` implemented-unverified · `100` verified.

**Remarks:** date + what was done / what is missing / bug or library reference. This is the home for bugs and change notes — do not spawn a separate file. Visual-gate failures are prefixed `⚠ visual:`; security findings `⚠ SECURITY`.

## UI / Pages

<!-- UI REQs are built by /trblazeui from the approved mockups (docs/TfLens-UIDesign.md
     + docs/mockups/*.html); every REQ cites the mockup screen it realizes. The mockups
     are a click-through — start at docs/mockups/login.html. Control names below come
     from the UIDesign component maps; §Library gaps there mandates StatTile/StatGroup
     for KPI cards, PasswordStrength for password meters, CodeBlock for compare output
     and CenteredPanel for the auth cards — use those, not the fallbacks. -->

### Page: Login (`/login`)

<a id="d-req-ui-001"></a>
- **REQ-UI-001** — Anonymous split-layout login page: left brand panel, right `CenteredPanel` + `Card` with email and password `Field`s, full-width Sign in button, links to Register and Forgot password, and a muted "GitHub sign-in: coming in a later release" line with **no GitHub button** in this release (BRD-1, BRD-90, BRD-94). *Mockup:* docs/mockups/login.html.
  - *Acceptance:* page renders anonymously at `/login` with `data-testid` `login-email`, `login-pass`, `login-submit`; Enter submits; the submit button shows a `Spinner` and disables while the AppManager call is in flight; a failed sign-in shows the generic `Alert Variant=Danger` "Sign-in failed. Check your email and password." with the fields' values kept and the AppManager error code never rendered; `ACCOUNT_LOCKED` is the only specific message ("Account locked — try again later"); no GitHub/SSO button exists in the DOM; controls do not overlap at desktop + mobile widths (visual gate).

### Page: Register (`/register`)

<a id="d-req-ui-002"></a>
- **REQ-UI-002** — Anonymous registration page on the same split layout: first name, last name (2-col grid), email, password with a `PasswordStrength` meter and the rule description, confirm password, an always-visible `Alert Variant=Info AccentBorder` "Every TfLens account is a Manager — no licence, no subscription.", Create account button and a link back to Sign in (BRD-91, BRD-95). *Mockup:* docs/mockups/register.html.
  - *Acceptance:* `data-testid` `reg-first`, `reg-last`, `reg-email`, `reg-pass`, `reg-confirm`, `reg-submit` present; the AppManager password rules (8+ chars, uppercase, digit, special) are checked locally and shown as per-rule `FieldError`s **before** any API call; mismatched confirm shows "passwords differ"; an AppManager duplicate email shows `FieldError` "already registered"; `VALIDATION_ERROR` / `DECRYPTION_FAILED` render as one generic danger `Alert`; the Manager note is always visible; controls do not overlap at desktop + mobile widths (visual gate).

### Page: Forgot password (`/forgot-password`)

<a id="d-req-ui-003"></a>
- **REQ-UI-003** — Anonymous forgot-password page: one email `Field`, Send reset link button, and a success `Alert Variant=Success` that **replaces the form**, plus a "Back to sign in" link (BRD-92). *Mockup:* docs/mockups/forgot-password.html.
  - *Acceptance:* `data-testid` `forgot-email`, `forgot-submit`; after submit the page **always** shows "If that address exists, a reset link is on its way." regardless of whether the address exists (enumeration-safe — no timing or wording difference); the form is replaced, not merely disabled; controls do not overlap at desktop + mobile widths (visual gate).

### Page: Reset password (`/reset-password`)

<a id="d-req-ui-004"></a>
- **REQ-UI-004** — Anonymous reset-password page reading `?token=…`: new password with `PasswordStrength`, confirm, Reset button; an invalid/expired token replaces the form with `Alert Variant=Danger`; success shows `Alert Variant=Success` "Password updated." plus a Sign in button (BRD-92). *Mockup:* docs/mockups/reset-password.html.
  - *Acceptance:* `data-testid` `reset-pass`, `reset-confirm`, `reset-submit`; the same local password rules as Register are enforced inline; `INVALID_RESET_TOKEN` and `APP_ID_MISMATCH` both render "This reset link is invalid or has expired."; the token is read from the query string and never displayed; controls do not overlap at desktop + mobile widths (visual gate).

### Page: Profile (`/profile`)

<a id="d-req-ui-005"></a>
- **REQ-UI-005** — Authenticated profile page inside the shell: two side-by-side `Card`s — a read-only AppManager profile (`Avatar` initials, Email, Name, Role `Badge` Manager, Member since, Identity provider) and a change-password form (current / new / confirm with `PasswordStrength`) — plus a standing `Alert Variant=Info` "TfLens stores no passwords — your account lives in AppManager." (BRD-107, BRD-95). *Mockup:* docs/mockups/profile.html (user menu shown open).
  - *Acceptance:* `data-testid` `pw-current`, `pw-new`, `pw-confirm`, `pw-submit`; profile values come from `GET /UserSvc/profile` and show `Skeleton` while loading; `INVALID_CURRENT_PASSWORD` renders as a `FieldError` on the current-password field; success raises a toast; the Role badge always reads `Manager`; cards stack to one column under `md` and controls do not overlap at desktop + mobile widths (visual gate).

### Layout: App shell (sidebar + header)

- **REQ-UI-006** — `SidebarProvider` + `Sidebar Collapsible` shell with `SidebarHeader` (brand), `SidebarRail` and `SidebarTrigger`; navigation in the fixed working order **Repos** (`git-branch`, with `SidebarMenuBadge` repo count) then the Reports group — Coverage / health (`activity`), Three questions (`circle-question-mark`), Harness comparison (`git-compare`), Routing & economics (`route`), **Misses & rework (`bug`)**, Snapshot export (`download`); no separate Playbook item (BRD-5, BRD-105 — amended 2026-08-28 by BRD-112..BRD-130, which inserted *Misses & rework* between *Routing & economics* and *Snapshot export*). *(Icon corrected 2026-08-28: `help-circle` is an alias Lucide does not resolve — TR-008 — and rendered nothing; the code uses the canonical `circle-question-mark`.)* *Mockup:* docs/mockups/coverage.html (shell visible on every report mockup).
  - *Acceptance:* the **seven** items appear in exactly that order with those Lucide icons; `SidebarTrigger` collapses to an icon-only rail where each item shows its `Tooltip`; the collapsed/expanded state survives a reload via `CookieKey="tflens:sidebar"`; `IsActive` marks the current route; no `/playbook` nav item exists; controls do not overlap at desktop + mobile widths (visual gate).

  - *Acceptance:* the switch renders on all **six** report pages (Coverage, Three questions, Harness, Routing, **Misses & rework**, Snapshot export) and nowhere else; the selection persists per user across navigation and reload; switching re-queries the page rather than filtering client-side; each trigger's badge shows that framework's connected-repo count for this user; controls do not overlap at desktop + mobile widths (visual gate).

### Page: Repos (`/repos`)

<a id="d-req-ui-011"></a>
- **REQ-UI-011** — Repos page: header row (title + primary **Connect repo** button), a `StatGroup`/`StatTile` KPI row (Connected repos · Records synced · Last successful sync), and a `DataTable` of the user's repos with columns Repo (owner/name + GitHub link) · Branch · Kind `Badge` · Visibility `Badge` public · Status `Badge` · Last sync · Records · Actions (row Sync and Remove icon buttons), plus an `Empty` state for a new user (BRD-98). *Mockup:* docs/mockups/repos.html.
  - *Acceptance:* `data-testid` `repos-table`, `connect-repo`, `repos-empty`, `repo-sync-{name}`, `repo-remove-{name}`; the table shows only the signed-in user's repos; a repo in error shows a `Tooltip` carrying its `LastError` text; the row Sync button shows a `Spinner` while running; a user with no repos sees the `Empty` component with a working Connect action instead of an empty grid; controls do not overlap at desktop + mobile widths (visual gate).

<a id="d-req-ui-012"></a>
- **REQ-UI-012** — Connect-repo `Dialog`: GitHub URL or `owner/name` input, optional branch, Kind `Select` (Auto-detect / techieflow / playbook), a **Validate** button, a three-line validation result (Repository exists · Public · Telemetry path found: …), and a Connect button enabled **only after a green validation** (BRD-99, BRD-100). *Mockup:* docs/mockups/repos.html (dialog panel).
  - *Acceptance:* `data-testid` `connect-input`, `connect-validate`, `connect-submit`; Connect stays disabled until all three checks pass; a private repo shows `Alert Variant=Warning` "Private repos aren't supported in this release"; a missing telemetry path shows `Alert Variant=Danger`; a GitHub rate-limit response shows `Alert Variant=Warning` "GitHub rate limit reached — try again in N minutes"; on success the dialog shows a "Connecting…" `Progress`, the first sync runs, a toast reports the outcome and the sidebar Repos badge updates; controls do not overlap at desktop + mobile widths (visual gate).

<a id="d-req-ui-013"></a>
- **REQ-UI-013** — Remove-repo `AlertDialog` titled "Remove owner/name?" whose description states plainly that the parsed rows and raw archive for this repo are deleted and the GitHub repo is untouched, with a destructive Remove action (BRD-101). *Mockup:* docs/mockups/repos.html (dialog panel).
  - *Acceptance:* removal only proceeds through the confirm action (Escape/Cancel aborts with no data change); the description names the repo being removed; after confirmation the row disappears and the KPI counts update; controls do not overlap at desktop + mobile widths (visual gate).

<a id="d-req-ui-040"></a>
- **REQ-UI-040** — The Add-source `Dialog` on `/repos` opens on a **mode fork** — `Tabs` (or a segmented `TabsList`) with **Fetch via API** and **Import metric files** — and the import mode carries: source name, framework `Select`, optional `project_type` override, a drop zone accepting `.zip` / `.jsonl` / `.ndjson`, and a **preview panel that must render before the Import button enables** (records per stream, date range, invalid lines, unknown field names, bundle sha256) (BRD-131, BRD-138, BRD-140). *Mockup:* docs/mockups/repos.html (dialog panels).
  - *Acceptance:* `data-testid` `source-mode`, `import-drop`, `import-preview`, `import-submit`; **nothing is written until Import is pressed** — a cancelled or abandoned preview leaves zero rows and zero archive files; a `.zip` of `docs/metrics/` and a set of loose `.jsonl` files both produce the same preview; an unrecognised bundle, an unsafe archive and a **precomputed rollup / snapshot** are each refused with a message naming what to upload instead, and the Import button stays disabled; the private-repo refusal in Fetch-via-API mode offers a switch to the import mode inline rather than dead-ending (BRD-100); controls do not overlap at desktop + mobile widths (visual gate).

<a id="d-req-ui-041"></a>
- **REQ-UI-041** — The repos `DataTable` gains a **Source** column rendering `Badge` **Synced** (`api`) or **Imported** (`import`), and the per-row action differs by source kind: **Sync** (`refresh-cw`) for a fetched source, **Re-import** (`upload`) for an imported one — never a disabled Sync button, which would be a lie about what the row can do (BRD-98, BRD-132, BRD-135). *Mockup:* docs/mockups/repos.html.
  - *Acceptance:* `data-testid` `repos-table`, `repo-source-{name}`, `repo-reimport-{name}`; an imported row shows **no** Sync control anywhere; pressing header **Sync now** leaves every imported row's counts and timestamps untouched; the Last-sync column reads *last import* for an imported row; Remove behaves and warns identically for both kinds.

<a id="d-req-ui-042"></a>
- **REQ-UI-042** — Coverage shows the source badge per repo card and reads staleness by source kind: days since newest record for a fetched source (with the hook/pushing diagnosis, BRD-41), **days since import** for an imported one, worded *"this source can't refresh itself — re-import to update"* and **without** the hook diagnosis (BRD-136, BRD-137). *Mockup:* docs/mockups/coverage.html.
  - *Acceptance:* `data-testid` `repo-source-badge-{name}`; an imported source that is simply old does **not** turn the summary badge red on the hook rule, and the `update-framework.sh` advice never appears on it — it would be advice about a clone TfLens cannot see; the export's per-repo block carries `source_kind`.
